13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
Postmarketing Requirements (PMRs)
PREA PMR 3980-1
Conduct a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group, safety and efficacy
study of Azstarys in male and female children 4 to 12 years of age diagnosed with ADHD.
Randomization should be stratified by two age groups, i.e., 4 to less than 6 years and 6 to 12
years of age. Also, this study must include sparse pharmacokinetic (PK) sampling in children ages
4 to less than 6 years to characterize the shape of the PK curve in this population.
PREA PMR 3980-2
Conduct a 12-month, open-label study to obtain information on safety and tolerability of
Azstarys in male and female children 4 to less than 6 years of age diagnosed with ADHD.

Figure 20: Proposed Metabolic Pathway of SDX

Source: Applicant’s Figure, Pharmacokinetics Written Summary, p. 55

Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
Cross-Species Comparison: Because of the species difference for PK/TK for SDX and d-MPH
release, the Applicant conducted a cross-species analysis for humans, dogs, rats, and rabbits
using single-dose data from multiple clinical studies, 14-day and 1-month rat and dog repeat
dose-studies, and a single-dose PK study in rabbit. Relative exposure ratios (RR) between
species using dose normalized AUC values were used to help determine the most appropriate
species (Table 46). The results showed that, exposures to SDX relative to d-MPH in humans is
lower than in rabbits and dogs, but higher than in rats, suggesting that rabbits and dogs are
better models than rats for assessing SDX toxicity. Comparing dogs and rabbits, suggest that
even though dogs have greater SDX exposures than rabbits they have even greater d-MPH
exposures compared to rabbits. This suggested that rabbits may tolerate higher doses of SDX
per body weight (or surface area) than dogs if comparable d-MPH exposure produces
comparable pharmacodynamic responses in dogs and rabbits.

Histopathology
Liver
Minimal-to-moderate hepatocellular pigmentation occurred in a dose-dependent manner in
test article-dosed groups (Table 52) and was still observed after the recovery period (Table 53).
Hepatocellular pigmentation was also seen in a small number of male and female control
animals at the end of dosing and in d-MPH dosed animals at the end of dosing and after the
recovery period. The incidence and severity were similar for MD and d-MPH females (150
mg/kg KP415 is equimolar to 75 mg/kg d-MPH HCl); however, were lower for d-MPH males
compared to all dose groups at the end of dosing but were similar at the end of the recovery
period. According to the Applicant the “…pigmentation was characterized by subtle, punctate,
finely granular, goldenbrown, circumscribed, non-refractile cytoplasmic structures that were
approximately 0.25-1 μm in size; this intracellular pigmented material was most common in
periportal hepatocytes but could occasionally be found widespread throughout all
hepatocellular zones, particularly with increasing severity grade.” There was no evidence of
other hepatocellular findings (including degeneration, necrosis, or hypertrophy) and there were
no changes in liver weights or clinical chemistry parameters which correlated to the
pigmentation.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation: NDA 212994
Azstarys (serdexmethylphenidate and dexmethylphenidate) capsules, for oral use
Table 52: Summary of Liver Histopathology Findings at the End of Dosing (PND 197) in
Chronic Juvenile Animal Study

Source: Applicant’s Table, Study No. 20144952, p.69
Table 53: Summary of Liver Histopathology Findings after the Recovery Period (PND 224) in
Chronic Juvenile Animal Study

Source: Applicant’s Table, Study No. 20144952, p.70
To evaluate the punctate granular pigmentation within hepatocytes, staining for iron (Perl’s
Prussian Blue), bile (Hall’s Bilirubin), and lipofuscin (Schmorl’s) was conducted on a subset of
animals at the end of dosing and after the recovery period (n = 3/control, HD, and d-MPH
groups; Table 54 and Table 55). There was no staining of punctate liver granules for iron or bile.
There was partial staining of punctate liver granules by Schmorl’s stain. Granules varied from
light blue to dark blue and there was heterogeneity of staining, where some granules did not
stain and other granules stained light-to-dark blue (some were adjacent within the same cell).
According to the Applicant, this equivocal staining pattern may suggest, but is not definitive for
lipofuscin. The staining pattern was similar for all dose groups evaluated (except male controls
at the end of dosing).

1. Executive Summary

The applicant, KemPharm Inc., submitted the results from three human abuse potential studies
KP415-a01 (oral), KP415-a02 (intranasal), and Study KP415-a03 (intravenous) for the assessment
of abuse potential of KP415 prodrug.
1.1. Study KP415-a01
The study was a phase 1, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, single-dose, five-way
crossover study to determine the abuse potential and pharmacokinetics of 120 and 240 mg doses of
KP415 prodrug in capsules compared with 80 mg Focalin XR, 60 mg phentermine and placebo after
oral administration in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users. Fifty subjects were
randomized to the Treatment Phase. Of these, 45 (90%) subjects completed the study.
The primary analysis demonstrated the significantly lower mean of Drug Liking Emax scores
produced by both doses of KP415 prodrug compared to that by 80 mg Focalin XR with a test
margin of 10 points (p≤ 0.0085). However, the study failed to demonstrate no significant mean
difference in maximum liking between each dose of KP415 prodrug and placebo (p≥0.0567). The
study was validated by the significantly larger mean produced by 80 mg Focalin XR than that by
placebo with a test margin 15 (p<0.0001).
The results from the analysis on the secondary endpoints, Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take
Drug Again Emax, demonstrated significant difference in means between 80 mg Focalin XR and
placebo (p≤0.0438). However, the results did not support the findings in the primary analysis for the
comparisons between each dose of KP415 prodrug and 80 mg Focalin XR (p≥0.0873). It was
because 11 (24.4%) and 10 (22.2%) subjects in some degree overall disliked 80 mg Focalin XR and
did not want to take 80 mg Focalin XR again, respectively. The means of Overall Drug Liking
Emax and Take Drug Again Emax were 63.0 and 64.6 produced by 80 mg Focalin XR,
respectively, and the means produced by both doses of KP415 prodrug were within [56.9, 58.5] for
both secondary endpoints. The descriptive statistics on the exploratory measures (Any Effects VAS,
Bad Effects VAS, Good Effects VAS, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, and High VAS) and their Emax
scores supported the findings from the primary analysis for these comparisons.
The comparisons between 60 mg phentermine and each dose of KP415 prodrug were prespecified
as exploratory comparisons. These comparisons showed that both doses of KP415 prodrug
produced means significantly smaller than 60 mg phentermine for the primary and secondary
endpoints (p≤0.0195) except the comparison between 60 mg phentermine and 240 mg KP415
prodrug for Drug Liking Emax. Note that this comparison used a test margin of 10 points (In
general, sponsors used a test margin of zero for the comparison between a schedule IV positive
control and test drug). The p-value of the test was 0.0664, and the lower confidence limit of this
comparison was 9.6. The significantly larger mean produced by 60 mg phentermine than that by
placebo with a test margin of 15 points validated the exploratory comparisons ( p=0.0032). The
descriptive statistics of exploratory endpoints supported that both doses of KP415 prodrug had less
abuse potential compared to 60 mg phentermine.

6

Reference ID: 4739635

In conclusion, Study KP415-a01 demonstrated that with oral administration 120 and 240 mg doses
of KP415 prodrug had less abuse potential compared to not only 80 mg Focalin XR but also 60 mg
phentermine. However, the study did not demonstrate that both doses of KP415 prodrug had no
abuse potential compared to placebo.
1.2. Study KP415-a02
The study was a phase 1, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, single-dose,
3-Way crossover study to determine the abuse potential and pharmacokinetics of 80 mg KP415
prodrug API compared with 40 mg d-Methylphenidate API and placebo after intranasal
administration in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users. Forty-nine subjects were
randomized to the Treatment Phase. Of these, 45 (91.8%) subjects completed the study.
The primary analysis demonstrated that for Drug Liking Emax, the mean difference between 40 mg
d-MPH API and 80 mg KP415 prodrug API was significantly larger than 10 points (p<0.0001).
However, the study failed to demonstrate no significant median difference between 40 mg KP415
prodrug API and placebo with a test margin of 11 points (p=0.9998). The median difference
between 40 mg d-MPH API and placebo significantly greater than 15 points validated the study
(p<0.0001).
The results from the secondary analysis showed that for both Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take
Drug Again Emax, the median difference between 40 mg d-MPH API and placebo, the mean
difference between 40 mg d-MPH API and 80 mg KP415 prodrug API, and the median difference
between 80 mg KP415 prodrug API and placebo were significantly greater than zero (p≤0.0027).
The descriptive statistics for the exploratory measures (Any Effects VAS, Bad Effects VAS, Good
Effects VAS, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, High VAS) and their Emax scores supported the findings
from the primary and secondary analyses.
All subjects, including those who discontinued early, were able to completely insufflate the study
treatments. The means for Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5 minutes post-dose were 19.1, 65.8 and 6.9
for 40 mg d-MPH API, 80 mg KP415 prodrug API and placebo, respectively. The standard
deviation was larger produced by 80 mg KP415 prodrug API (32.0) compared to those by both 40
mg d-MPH API (23.5) and placebo (18.8). The correlation of Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5
minutes post-dose and Drug Liking Emax and that of Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5 minutes postdose and Take Drug Again Emax produced by 80 mg KP415 prodrug API were small (0.1252 and 0.2598, respectively), which indicates that the difficulty of nasal insufflation at 5 minutes post-dose
produced by 80 mg KP415 prodrug API may not be the main reason for reducing the abuse
potential of 80 mg KP415 prodrug API compared to 40 mg d-MPH API.
In conclusion, Study KP415-a02 demonstrated that with intranasal administration 80 mg KP415
prodrug API had less abuse potential than 40 mg d-MPH API and had greater abuse potential
compared to placebo.

7

Reference ID: 4739635

1.3. Study KP415-a03
The study was a Phase 1, double-blind, single-dose, 3-treatment, 3-period, 6-sequence, randomized
crossover study to compare the abuse potential and pharmacokinetics of 30 mg KP415 API with 15
mg d-methylphenidate and placebo administered intravenously in healthy, nondependent,
recreational stimulant users. A total of 31 subjects were randomized to the Treatment Phase. Thirty
(96.8%) subjects completed the study.
The primary analysis demonstrated that the mean of Drug Liking Emax scores produced by 30 mg
KP415 API was significantly smaller than that by 15 mg d-MPH API with a test margin of 10
points. (p<0.0001); and the median difference in maximum liking between 30 mg KP415 API and
placebo was significantly smaller than 11 points (p=0.0040). The mean difference in maximum
liking between 15 mg d-MPH API and placebo was significantly greater than 15 points (p<0.0001).
Thus, the study was validated.
The key secondary endpoint, Take Drug Again Emax, was on a unipolar visual analog scale. Unlike
studies KP415-a01 and KP415-a02, in which Take Drug Again VAS was on a bipolar visual analog
scale and the test margins for secondary endpoints were zero, the same test margins used for Drug
Liking Emax (bipolar) were used in the analysis on this key secondary endpoint in this study. In
other words, the prespecified test margins did not take unipolar scale of this measure into account.
The secondary analysis on the key secondary endpoint demonstrated significantly smaller mean
produced by 30 mg KP415 API than that by 15 mg d-MPH with a test margin of 10 points
(p=0.0001) but failed to demonstrate no significant mean difference between 30 mg KP415 API and
placebo with a test margin of 11 points (p=0.2750). However, descriptive statistics on secondary
measures Any Effects VAS, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, Bed Effects VAS, Good Effects VAS,
High VAS, and Overall Drug Liking VAS and on their Emax scores all supported the findings from
the primary analysis.
In conclusion, Study PK415-a03 demonstrated that with IV administration, 30 mg KP415 API had
less abuse potential than 15 mg d-MPH API and did not have abuse potential compared to placebo.
NOTE: Per medical officer Shalini Bansil’s request, the reviewer also performed analysis on High
Emax for each study. These results along with descriptive statistics for ARCI Amphetamine Group
and ARCI Benzedrine Group are presented in Appendix.
The secondary analysis demonstrated that for the key secondary endpoint, the mean produced by 30
mg KP415 API was significantly smaller than that by 15 mg d-MPH with a test margin of 10 points
(p=0.0001) but failed to demonstrate no significant difference from that by placebo with a test
margin of 11 points (p=0.2750).

8

Reference ID: 4739635

2. Review report on Study KP415-a01
2.1. Overview
Study KP415-a01 was a phase 1, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, single-dose, fiveway crossover study to determine the abuse potential and pharmacokinetics of 120 and 240 mg
KP415 prodrug in capsules compared with 80 mg of Focalin XR and 60 mg of phentermine after
oral administration in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
2.1.1. Objectives of the study
Primary Objective:
• To assess the abuse potential of KP415 prodrug in capsules at maximum therapeutic and
supratherapeutic doses relative to Focalin XR, phentermine, and placebo when administered
orally in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
• To assess the pharmacokinetics (PK) of intact KP415 prodrug and KP415 prodrug-derived
d-methylphenidate (d-MPH) after maximum therapeutic and supratherapeutic single oral
doses of KP415 prodrug in capsules in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
Secondary Objectives:
• To assess the safety and tolerability of maximum therapeutic and supratherapeutic single
oral doses of KP415 prodrug in capsules in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant
users.
• To assess the PK of l-methylphenidate (l-MPH) (if applicable) and ritalinic acid after
maximum therapeutic and supratherapeutic single oral doses of KP415 prodrug in capsules
in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.2.1.2. Study design
The study was a Phase 1, randomized, double-blind, single-dose, active-and placebo-controlled, 5treatment, 5-period, 10-sequence crossover study evaluating the abuse potential and PK of 120 mg
and 240 mg KP415 prodrug in capsules, extended-release d-MPH (Focalin XR 80 mg), 60 mg
phentermine and placebo, after oral administration in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant
users.
The study consisted of a Screening Period, an in-clinic Drug Discrimination Phase, an in-clinic
Treatment Phase, and a Follow-up Visit.
Qualification Phase
Screening Period: Subjects underwent a Screening Period up to 28 days prior to entering the Drug
Discrimination Phase of the study.
Drug Discrimination Phase (Qualification Phase): Subjects who successfully completed the
Screening Period returned to the clinic to complete the Drug Discrimination Phase. The Drug
Discrimination Test was performed to ensure that subjects could differentiate between the effects of
a single dose of Focalin XR and placebo administered orally. Subjects who successfully completed
the Drug Discrimination Phase and who qualified for the Treatment Phase returned to the research

9

Reference ID: 4739635

clinic to enter the Treatment Phase. The Drug Discrimination Phase was a double-blind, oral,
single-dose, 2-treatment, 2-period, 2-sequence, randomized, crossover design. Subjects received
single oral doses of the following treatments separated by a 48-hour washout period:
• Treatment X: 80 mg Focalin XR (2 x 40 mg capsules, overencapsulated).
• Treatment Y: Placebo (2 matching placebo capsules).
Abuse potential assessments were performed at different times after the administration of study
drug. Safety assessments were performed throughout and at the end of the Drug Discrimination
Phase. A subject was eligible for the Treatment Phase only if all the eligibility criteria for the Drug
Discrimination Phase, based on the drug effects and tolerability assessments, were met. The
washout period between the last dose of study drug in the Drug Discrimination Phase and the first
dose of study drug in the Treatment Phase was at least 72 hours and no more than 35 days
(including a 33-day outpatient period).
Treatment Phase
After subjects completed and passed the Drug Discrimination Phase, they were eligible to enter into
the Treatment Phase. The Treatment Phase was a double-blind, oral, single-dose, 5-treatment, 5period, 10-sequence, randomized, crossover design, with the following treatments, each separated
by a minimum 96-hour washout period. The treatments were
Treatment A: 120 mg KP415 prodrug (2 x 60 mg capsule) (test product)
Treatment B: 240 mg KP415 prodrug (4 x 60 mg capsules) (test product)
Treatment C: 80 mg Focalin XR (2 x 40 mg capsules) (primary positive control)
Treatment D: 60 mg phentermine (2 x 30 mg capsules) (secondary positive control)
Treatment E: Placebo (matching capsules)
Reviewer’s comments: Note that in the dataset, Treatments A, B, C, D and E denote 80 mg Focalin
XR, 120 mg KP415 prodrug, 240 mg KP415 prodrug, Placebo, and Phentermine, respectively.
Abuse potential measures and PK was collected at different times after the administration of study
drug. Safety assessments were performed.
Follow-up Period
Subjects who successfully completed the Drug Discrimination Phase but were not eligible for the
Treatment Phase, attended a Follow-up Visit 7 ±2 days after administration of the last dose of study
drug in the Drug Discrimination Phase. Subjects who successfully completed the Treatment Phase
attended a Follow-up Visit 7 ±2 days after administration of the last dose of study drug in the
Treatment Phase. Safety assessments were performed.
2.1.3. Study Subjects
A total of 230 subjects were screened, of whom 112 passed screening and were enrolled in the
Qualification Phase of the study. Sixty-two of the subjects in the Qualification Phase were
discontinued prior to the Treatment Phase. Fifty subjects enrolled in the treatment phase. Forty five

10

Reference ID: 4739635

of these subjects completed the study. The dispositive of subjects shows in the sponsor’s Figure 1 in
the study report.

Eligibility Criteria to Enter Treatment Phase
Drug Liking scores and other HAP PD measures in the Drug Discrimination Phase were
used to determine whether subjects could discriminate between Focalin XR and placebo,
and, in combination with safety and tolerability data, whether subjects would be eligible for
the Treatment Phase according to the following criteria:
1. Peak score (Emax) of at least 65 points for Focalin XR on the Drug Liking Visual Analog
Scale (VAS) and at least 15 points higher in response to Focalin XR versus placebo

11

Reference ID: 4739635

response (so that subjects have a minimal response to Focalin XR, outside the placebo
response range).
2. Placebo response is in the range of 40 to 60 points (inclusive) on the Drug Liking VAS (so
that subjects neither endorsed liking nor disliking of placebo).
3. Acceptable safety and tolerability after oral administration of the Focalin XR treatment.
4. Acceptable overall responses to Focalin XR and placebo on all other HAP PD measures, as
judged by the Investigator or designee.
2.1.4. Abuse potential endpoints
Primary Endpoint
• Drug Liking Emax (data collection time point from 0.5 hours up to 24 hours postdose) on a
bipolar 0- to 100-point Visual Analog Scale (VAS).
Secondary Endpoints
• Take Drug Again Emax (data collection time at 12 and 24 hours postdose) on a bipolar 0-to
100-point VAS.
• Overall Drug Liking Emax (data collection time at 12 and 24 hours postdose) on a bipolar
0-100- point VAS.
Exploratory endpoints
Emax for Feeling High, Good Effects, Bad Effects, Any Effects, and for Drowsiness/Alertness VAS
scores, and for (ARCI)-A and ARCI-BG scores; and time of maximum effect (TEmax) and AUEs
for Drug Liking.
2.1.5. Statistical methodologies used in the Sponsor’s analyses
Primary hypotheses for the primary endpoint Drug Liking Emax
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐸 ≤ 15 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐸 > 15 (80 mg Focalin XR versus Placebo)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐴 ≤ 10 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐴 > 10 (80 mg Focalin XR versus 120 mg KP415)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐵 ≤ 10 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐵 > 10 (80 mg Focalin XR versus 240 mg KP415)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐴 − 𝜇𝐸 ≥ 11 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐴 − 𝜇𝐸 < 11 (120 mg KP415 versus Placebo)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐵 − 𝜇𝐸 ≥ 11 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐵 − 𝜇𝐸 < 11 (240 mg KP415 versus Placebo)
Exploratory hypotheses
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐸 ≤ 10 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐸 > 10 (60 mg phentermine versus placebo)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐴 ≤ 10 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐴 > 10 (60 mg phentermine versus 120 mg KP415)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐵 ≤ 10 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐵 > 10 (60 mg phentermine versus 240 mg KP415)
Reviewer’s comments: In the reviewer’s analysis the test margin of 15 is used for the validation test
for phentermine 60 mg.
The SAS mixed effects linear model procedure (PROC MIXED) will be used to construct an
ANOVA model for the primary endpoint, Drug Liking Emax. The model will include terms for

12

Reference ID: 4739635

treatment, period, sequence and first-order carryover effect (‘priortrt’) as fixed effects, with subject
nested within sequence as a random effect. If the carryover effect is found to be non-significant at
the 25% level, this term will be dropped from the model. If carryover effect is found to be
significant at the 25% level, indicator variables (one for each treatment in the model) for ‘prior
treatment’ will be created to maintain the first-order carryover effect variable without confounding
the effects of the treatment, period and sequence variables.
The residuals of the primary endpoint will be investigated for normality using the Shapiro-Wilk W
test. Parameters will be analyzed under the assumption of a normal distribution if the p-value of the
test is ≥0.01.
Reviewer’s comments: Even though the sponsor set priortrt for carryover effects in the dataset, the
results of the primary analysis was not from a model included priortrt. In fact, the carryover effect
was significant in the primary analysis.
If the probability value is <0.01 for the Shapiro Wilk W test on the residuals from the mixed model,
and the distribution of the paired differences are deemed unskewed (-1.5< skewness value <1.5),
then Drug Liking Emax will be analyzed using paired t-tests for each treatment comparison.
If the distribution of the paired differences is skewed (skewness value < -1.5 or skewness value >
1.5), then Drug Liking Emax will be analyzed non-parametrically. The Sign Test will be used to
evaluate treatment differences.
Reviewer’s comments: The reviewer only reviewed the SAP for Study KP415-a02 and commented
that “If the skewness of the distribution is between -1.5 and 1.5, a paired t-test will be used.” is not
acceptable.
Hypotheses for all secondary and exploratory endpoints
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐸 = 0 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐸 ≠ 0 (80 mg Focalin XR versus Placebo)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐴 = 0 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐴 ≠ 0 (80 mg Focalin XR versus 120 mg KP415)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐵 = 0 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐶 − 𝜇𝐵 ≠ 0 (80 mg Focalin XR versus 240 mg KP415)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐴 − 𝜇𝐸 = 0 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐴 − 𝜇𝐸 ≠ 0 (120 mg KP415 versus Placebo)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐵 − 𝜇𝐸 = 0 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐵 − 𝜇𝐸 ≠ 0 (240 mg KP415 versus Placebo)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐴 = 0 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐴 ≠ 0 (60 mg phentermine versus 120 mg KP415)
𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐵 = 0 versus 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐷 − 𝜇𝐵 ≠ 0 (80 mg phentermine versus 240 mg KP415)
where μ is the mean response across subjects (if nonparametric analyses are necessary, the median
of the differences will be tested rather than the difference in means between treatments); all tests
will be two-sided with a significance level of 0.05 (95% CIs will be reported, as appropriate),
except for the comparison between KP415 and Placebo, which will be performed as two-sided
with a significance level of 0.1 (90% CIs will be reported, as appropriate). No adjustments will be
made for multiplicity.
The secondary parameters (Overall Drug Liking and Take Drug Again Emax) will be evaluated
with the same steps for model selection described for Drug Liking Emax.

13

Reference ID: 4739635

2.1.6. Sponsor’s Summary and Conclusions
•

•

•

•

•

The study met validity criteria for both Focalin XR and phentermine. The primary endpoint,
Drug Liking Emax, as well as most of the secondary and exploratory PD parameter
assessments, showed greater abuse potential for 80 mg Focalin XR compared to 120 mg
and 240 mg KP415 prodrug.
The primary endpoint did not show conclusively that either dose of KP415 prodrug has an
oral abuse potential that is non-inferior to placebo. For both doses, the difference in Drug
Liking Emax relative to placebo was lower than the equivalence margin of 11 points (the
upper 95% CIs for 120 and 240 mg KP415 prodrug were trivially [CI: 11.17] and slightly
[CI: 12.62] larger than the margin), but not statistically significantly lower. The results of
some of the secondary and exploratory PD parameters, conducted as confirmatory-type
hypotheses with no margins, suggested an oral abuse potential for KP415 prodrug that was
similar to placebo, including Overall Drug Liking, Take Drug Again, Any Effects (similar
at 120-mg dose only), High (120-mg dose only). Good Effects, Alertness/Drowsiness,
ARCI-A and ARCI-BG indicated greater abuse potential for both doses of KP415 prodrug
than for placebo.
A 60-mg phentermine dose showed greater oral abuse potential, based on analyses of Drug
Liking mean Emax, than the 120 mg dose of KP415 prodrug but not the 240 mg dose (the
lower 95% CI of the mean difference between phentermine and 240 mg KP415 prodrug
[CI: 9.62] was marginally below the 10-point superiority margin). The results of most of the
secondary and exploratory PD parameters supported an overall impression of a reduced
abuse potential for both doses of KP415 prodrug relative to 60 mg phentermine.
Absorption of intact KP415 prodrug was rapid (median Tmax, 2 and 3 hours for 120 mg
and 240 mg KP415 prodrug, respectively). Intact KP415 prodrug peak and total systemic
exposure increased 2.3-fold from 120 mg KP415 prodrug to 240 mg KP415 prodrug. The
median T1/2 of KP415 prodrug was dose independent (~6 hours for both treatments).
Median d-MPH Tmax was longer for both 120 and 240 mg KP415 prodrug (9 and 11 hours,
respectively) compared to Tmax1 for Focalin XR (Tmax1: 1.5 hours); median d-MPH T1/2
was also longer for 120 and 240 mg KP415 prodrug (10.4 and 10.9 hours, respectively)
compared to T1/2 for Focalin XR (3.8 hours).

2.3.1.2. Statistical Testing
For examining the abuse potential of KP415 prodrug, the reviewer studied the following
comparisons.
1. 80 mg Focalin XR versus Placebo
2. 120 mg KP415 prodrug versus 80 mg Focalin XR
3. 240 mg KP415 prodrug versus 80 mg Focalin XR
4. 120 mg KP415 prodrug versus P
5. 240 mg KP415 prodrug versus P
In addition, for exploration, the following comparisons are also considered.
6. 60 mg Phentermine versus Placebo
7. 120 mg KP415 prodrug versus 60 mg Phentermine
8. 240 mg KP415 prodrug versus 60 mg Phentermine
The statistical model used in the reviewer’s primary analysis was a mixed-effects model which
included sequence, period, treatment and the first-order-carryover as fixed effects, and subject as a
random effect. For the primary endpoint Drug Liking Emax, Levene’s test for homogeneity of
variances was not significant (p=0.1817). The first-order-carryover effect was significant
(p=0.0030 <0.05). Because the sponsor did not prespecify the first period analysis if the p-value of
the first-order-carryover effect was significant at 0.05 level, the reviewer kept the first-ordercarryover effect in the model. Nevertheless, the summary statistics based on data from first period
are presented in Table 2.

The results from the reviewer’s primary analysis on Drug Liking Emax showed that
1. 80 mg Focalin XR produced the mean response was significantly larger than placebo with a
test margin of 15 points (p<0.0001);
2. both 120 and 240 mg doses of KP415 prodrug produced mean responses were significantly
smaller than 80 mg Focalin XR with a test margin of 10 points (p≤0.0058);
3. both 120 and 240 mg doses of KP415 prodrug failed to demonstrate no significant
difference in mean compared to placebo with a test margin of 11 points (p≥0.0567).
The results from the reviewer’s exploratory comparisons on Drug Liking Emax showed that
1. 60 mg Phentermine produced mean response significantly larger than placebo with a test
margin of 15 points (p=0.0032);
2. 120 mg KP415 prodrug produced mean response significantly smaller than 60 mg
phentermine with a test margin of 10 points (p=0.0195);
3. 240 mg KP415 prodrug did not produce mean response significantly smaller than 60 mg
dose of phentermine with a test margin of 10 points (p=0.0664).

2.4. Conclusion
The primary analysis demonstrated the significantly lower mean of Drug Liking Emax scores
produced by both doses of KP415 prodrug compared to that by 80 mg Focalin XR with a test
margin of 10 points (p≤ 0.0085). However, the study failed to demonstrate no significant mean
difference in maximum liking between each dose of KP415 prodrug and placebo (p≥0.0567). The
study was validated by the significantly larger mean produced by 80 mg Focalin XR than that by
placebo with a test margin 15 (p<0.0001).
The results from the analysis on the secondary endpoints, Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take
Drug Again Emax, demonstrated significant difference in means between 80 mg Focalin XR and
placebo (p≤0.0438). However, the results did not support the findings in the primary analysis for the
comparisons between each dose of KP415 prodrug and 80 mg Focalin XR (p≥0.0873). It was
because 11 (24.4%) and 10 (22.2%) subjects in some degree overall disliked 80 mg Focalin XR and
did not want to take 80 mg Focalin XR again, respectively. The means of Overall Drug Liking
Emax and Take Drug Again Emax were 63.0 and 64.6 produced by 80 mg Focalin XR,
respectively, and the means produced by both doses of KP415 prodrug were within [56.9, 58.5] for
both secondary endpoints. The descriptive statistics on the exploratory measures (Any Effects VAS,
Bad Effects VAS, Good Effects VAS, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, and High VAS) and their Emax
scores supported the findings from the primary analysis for these comparisons.
The comparisons between 60 mg phentermine and each dose of KP415 prodrug were prespecified
as exploratory comparisons. These comparisons showed that both doses of KP415 prodrug
produced means significantly smaller than 60 mg phentermine for the primary and secondary
endpoints (p≤0.0195) except the comparison between 60 mg phentermine and 240 mg KP415 for
Drug Liking Emax. Note that this comparison used a test margin of 10 points (In general, sponsors
used a test margin of zero for the comparison between a schedule IV positive control and test drug).

26

Reference ID: 4739635

The p-value of the test was 0.0664, and the lower confidence limit of this comparison was 9.6. The
significantly larger mean produced by 60 mg phentermine than that by placebo with a test margin of
15 points validated the exploratory comparisons ( p=0.0032). The descriptive statistics of
exploratory endpoints supported that both doses of KP415 prodrug had less abuse potential
compared to 60 mg phentermine.
In conclusion, Study KP415-a01 demonstrated that with oral administration 120 and 240 mg doses
of KP415 prodrug had less abuse potential compared to not only 80 mg Focalin XR but also 60 mg
phentermine. However, the study did not demonstrate that both doses of KP415 prodrug had no
abuse potential compared to placebo.

27

Reference ID: 4739635

3. Review report on Study KP415-a02
3.1. Overview
Study KP415-a02 was a phase 1, randomized, double-Blind, placebo-controlled, single-dose,
3-Way crossover study to determine the abuse potential and pharmacokinetics of 80 mg KP415
Prodrug API compared with 40 mg of d-Methylphenidate API after intranasal administration in
healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
3.1.1. Objectives of the study
Primary Objectives
• To assess the abuse potential of KP415 prodrug API relative to d-MPH API when
administered intranasally in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
• To assess the PK of intact KP415 and KP415-derived d-MPH after a single IN dose of
KP415 prodrug API in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
Secondary Objectives
• To assess the safety and tolerability of a single IN dose of KP415 prodrug API in healthy,
nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
• To assess the PK of 1-MPH (if applicable) and ritalinic acid after a single IN dose of KP415
prodrug API in healthy, nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
3.1.2. Study design
The overall study design is presented in Figure 1. The study consisted of a Screening Period, an
in-clinic Drug Discrimination Phase, a Treatment Phase, and a Follow-up visit, as follows:
• Screening Period: Subjects underwent a screening period up to 28 days prior to entering the
Drug Discrimination Phase of the study.
• Drug Discrimination Phase: Subjects who successfully completed the screening period
returned to the clinic to complete the Drug Discrimination Phase. The Drug Discrimination
test ensured that subjects were able to differentiate between the effects of a single dose of dMPH API and placebo administered intranasally. Subjects who successfully completed the
Drug Discrimination Phase remained as inpatients to enter the Treatment Phase. The Drug
Discrimination Phase was a double-blind, IN, single-dose, 2-treatment, 2-period, 2-sequence,
randomized, crossover design. Subjects received single IN doses of the following treatments
separated by a 48-hour washout period:
– Treatment X: 40 mg d-MPH API mixed with 40 mg microcrystalline cellulose (MCC)
– Treatment Y: 80 mg matching placebo powder
All subjects were required to fast for at least 8 hours prior to each dose until approximately 4
hours after each dose. Abuse potential assessments were performed at different times after the
administration of study drug. Safety assessments were performed throughout and at the end of
the Drug Discrimination Phase. A subject was eligible for the Treatment Phase only if all the
eligibility criteria for the Drug Discrimination Phase, based on the drug effects and
tolerability assessments, were met. The washout period between the last dose of the study

28

Reference ID: 4739635

drug in the Drug Discrimination Phase and the first dose of study drug in the Treatment Phase
was approximately 70 to 72 hours.

•

Treatment Phase: After subjects completed and passed the Drug Discrimination Phase,
they were eligible to enter into the Treatment Phase. The Treatment Phase was a
double-blind, IN, single-dose, 3-treatment, 3-period, 6-sequence, randomized, crossover
design separated by a minimum 96-hour washout period, with the following treatments:
– Treatment A: 80 mg KP415 prodrug API
– Treatment B: 40 mg d-MPH API
– Treatment C: matching placebo powder
All subjects were required to fast for at least 8 hours prior to each dose until approximately 4
hours after each dose. Abuse potential measures and PK were collected at different times after
the administration of study drug. Safety assessments were performed throughout and at the end
of the Treatment Phase.

•

Follow-up visit: 7±2 days after administration of the last dose of study drug in the
Treatment Phase, subjects returned to the research clinic for a Follow-up visit to
evaluate safety parameters.

The following chart shows the overall study design. It is Figure 1 in the study report.

29

Reference ID: 4739635

Eligibility Criteria to Enter Treatment Phase
Drug Liking scores and other HAP PD measures in the Drug Discrimination Phase were used to
determine whether subjects could discriminate between d-MPH API and placebo, and, in
combination with safety and tolerability data, whether subjects were eligible to continue in the
Treatment Phase, according to the following criteria:
1. Peak score (Emax) of at least 65 points for d-MPH API on the Drug Liking visual analog
scale (VAS) and at least 15 points higher in response to d-MPH API vs placebo response
(so that subjects have a minimal response to d-MPH API, outside the placebo response
range).
2. Placebo response was in the range of 40 to 60 points (inclusive) on the Drug Liking VAS
(so that subjects neither endorsed liking nor disliking of placebo).
3. Acceptable overall responses to d-MPH API and placebo on all other HAP PD measures, as
judged by the investigator or designee.
4. Acceptable safety and tolerability after IN administration of the d-MPH API dose.
5. Subject was able to insufflate at least 95% of the drug product (d-MPH API and placebo).
The principal investigator, in collaboration with the sponsor and medical monitor, evaluated these
criteria for individual subjects and decided whether subjects were eligible for the Treatment Phase.
Subjects who did not meet these requirements were withdrawn from further study participation.
A subject was eligible for the Treatment Phase only if all the eligibility criteria were met at the end
of the Drug Discrimination Phase. On a case-by-case basis, a subject who met all of the above
criteria, with the exception of sporadic erroneous responses, may have been permitted into the
Treatment Phase, provided that the investigator or designee determined that the subject’s response
was made in error and that the subject could be successfully retrained for the Treatment Phase
regarding proper completion of that assessment.
3.1.3. Study subjects
A total of 154 subjects were screened. A total of 68 subjects were randomized to the Qualification
Phase. Of these 68 subjects, 50 subjects passed Qualification Phase and 49 subjects were
randomized to the Treatment Phase, and 45 subjects completed the study. The following chart of
disposition of subjects is Figure 3 in the study report.

30

Reference ID: 4739635

Note: This is Figure 3 in the study report.

31

Reference ID: 4739635

- TEmax and area under the effect curves (AUEs) for Drug Liking

Descriptive statistics only

- Emin, TEmin for Drug Liking

3.1.5. Statistical methodologies used in the Sponsor’s analyses
The statistical methodologies used in the Sponsor’s analyses in this study are the same as those in Study
KP415-A01. See section 2.1.5.
3.1.6. Sponsor’s Summary and Conclusions on Pharmacodynamic study
Primary analysis
• The median of the difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between d-MPH API 40 mg and placebo
was 45.0 and the lower bound of the 95% CI was 41.0, which was greater than the margin of 15
(P<0.0001); therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected, and the validity of the study was
confirmed.
• The mean difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between d MPH API 40 mg vs KP415 prodrug
API 80 mg was 22.3 and the lower bound of the 95% CI was 17.3, which was greater than the
margin of 10 (P<0.0001); therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected. These results demonstrated
that compared to d-MPH API 40 mg, KP415 prodrug API 80 mg (equimolar dose of d-MPH) was
significantly less liked by recreational stimulant users.
• The mean difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between KP415 prodrug API 80 mg vs placebo
was 19.9 and the upper limit of the 95% CI was 24. 6, which was greater than the margin of 11
(P=0.999); therefore, the null hypothesis was accepted. These results indicate that the subjective
drug liking of KP415 prodrug API 80 mg was not statistically similar to placebo.
Secondary analysis
For secondary endpoints of Overall Drug Liking and Take Drug Again, Emax values for KP415

32

Reference ID: 4739635

prodrug API 80 mg were significantly lower than for d-MPH API 40 mg (equimolar dose of
d-MPH) yet significantly higher than for placebo. These findings indicate that subjects liked
KP415 prodrug less overall and would be less willing to take KP415 prodrug again when compared
to d-MPH; however, subjects liked KP415 prodrug more overall and would be more willing to take
KP415 prodrug again when compared to placebo.
Exploratory analysis
• The profile of significant differences for the exploratory endpoints mirrored that of the primary
and secondary endpoints, where the rank order of positive abuse-related effects was d-MPH API
> KP415 prodrug API > placebo.
• For the majority of measures assessing positive drug effects (Good Effects and High), stimulantlike effects (ARCI-A, ARCI-BG), Drowsiness/Alertness VAS) and other effects (Any Effects
VAS), KP415 prodrug API engendered Emax values that were significantly lower than d-MPH
and significantly higher than placebo.
• Negative drug effects were low overall. Bad Effects VAS Emax was significantly higher for dMPH API 40 mg and KP415 prodrug API 80 mg compared with placebo; however, bad effects
were not significantly different between the active treatments.
• KP415 prodrug API 80 mg was reported to be significantly more difficult to insufflate than both
d-MPH API 40 mg and placebo, as assessed on the Ease of Insufflation scale.
Conclusions
Overall, the PD results indicate that positive subjective effects of IN KP415 prodrug API 80 mg
were significantly lower compared with d-MPH API 40 mg and significantly higher compared with
placebo. These findings suggest that KP415 prodrug API has lower abuse potential than d-MPH
API when administered by the IN route, but is not devoid of abuse potential.

3.3.1. Insufflation
All subjects, including those who discontinued early, were able to completely insufflate the study
treatments. However, 80 mg KP415 prodrug API was reported to be significantly more difficult to
insufflate than both 40 mg d-MPH API and placebo, as assessed on the Ease of Insufflation scale. Table 8
listed summary statistics for the Ease of Insufflation VAS. The data were collected at 5 minutes postdose.

The means for Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5 minutes post-dose were 18.1, 65.8 and 6.9 for d-MPH API

at 5 minutes post-dose and Take Drug Again Emax produced by KP415 prodrug API 80 mg were small
(0.1252 and -0.2598, respectively), which indicates that difficulty insufflation may not be the main reason
for reducing abuse potential of KP415 prodrug with intranasal administration.

3.3.1.2. Statistical testing
For examining the abuse potential of KP415 prodrug with nasal administration, the reviewer studied the
following comparisons:

was less than 0.0001. The normality of the paired differences was examined. Table 10 provides results
from the W-test for paired comparisons for Drug Liking Emax.

3.4. Conclusion
The primary analysis demonstrated that for Drug Liking Emax, the mean difference between 40 mg dMPH API and 80 mg KP415 prodrug API was significantly larger than 10 points (p<0.0001). However,
the study failed to demonstrate median difference between 40 mg KP415 prodrug API and placebo
significantly smaller than 11 points (p=0.9998). The median difference between 40 mg d-MPH API and
placebo significantly greater than 15 points validated the study (p<0.0001).
The results from the secondary analysis showed that for both Overall Drug Liking Emax and Take Drug
Again Emax, the median difference between 40 mg d-MPH API and placebo, the mean difference
between 40 mg d-MPH API and 80 mg KP415 prodrug API, and the median difference between 80 mg
KP415 prodrug API and placebo were significantly greater than zero (p≤0.0027).
The descriptive statistics for the exploratory measures (Any Effects VAS, Bad Effects VAS, Good Effects
VAS, Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, High VAS) and their Emax scores supported the findings from the
primary and secondary analyses.
All subjects, including those who discontinued early, were able to completely insufflate the study
treatments. The means for Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5 minutes post-dose were 19.1, 65.8 and 6.9 for
40 mg d-MPH API, 80 mg KP415 prodrug API and placebo, respectively. The standard deviation was
larger produced by 80 mg KP415 prodrug API (32.0) compared to those by both 40 mg d-MPH API
(23.5) and placebo (18.8). The correlation of Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5 minutes post-dose and Drug
Liking Emax and that of Ease of Nasal Insufflation at 5 minutes post-dose and Take Drug Again Emax
produced by 80 mg KP415 prodrug API were small (0.1252 and -0.2598, respectively), which indicates
that the difficulty of nasal insufflation produced by 80 mg KP415 prodrug API may not be the main
reason for reducing the abuse potential for 80 mg KP415 prodrug API compared to 40 mg d-MPH API.
In conclusion, Study KP415-a02 demonstrated that with intranasal administration 80 mg KP415 prodrug
API had less abuse potential than 40 mg d-MPH API and had greater abuse potential compared to
placebo.

43

Reference ID: 4739635

4. Review report on Study KP415-a03

4.1. Overview
KP415-a03 was a Phase 1, double-blind, single-dose, 3-treatment, 3-Period, 6-sequence, randomized
crossover study to compare the abuse potential and pharmacokinetics of KP415 API with dmethylphenidate API administered intravenoustly in healthy, nondependenct, recreational stimulant users.
4.1.1. Objectives of the study
Primary objectives
Cohort 1
• To determine the optimal dose of d-MPH API based on tolerability and HAP pharmacodynamic
(PD) assessments after a single IV injection in nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
Cohort 2
• To assess the abuse potential of KP415 API after a single IV injection in nondependent,
recreational stimulant users.
• To assess the PK of intact KP415, KP415-derived d-MPH, l-MPH (if applicable), and ritalinic
acid after a single IV injection of KP415 API in nondependent, recreational stimulant users.
Secondary objective for Cohort 2
To Assess the safety of KP415 API after a single IV injection in nondependent,
recreational stimulant users.
Reviewer’s comment: This review focused on the primary objectives for cohort 2 in the Part B of the
study.
4.1.2. Study design for Part B
Part B (Cohort 2) consisted of a Screening Period, a Drug Discrimination Phase (Qualification
Phase), a Treatment Phase and a Follow-Up Visit, as follows:
Screening Period: Subjects underwent a screening period up to 35 days prior to entering the Drug
Discrimination Phase.
Drug Discrimination Phase (Qualification Phase): Subjects who successfully completed the Screening
Period returned to the clinic to complete the Drug Discrimination Phase (Qualification Phase). The Drug
Discrimination Test was performed to ensure that subjects could differentiate between the effects of a
single dose of d-MPH API and placebo administered by IV injection. Subjects who successfully
completed the Drug Discrimination Test remained as inpatients to enter the Treatment Phase. The Drug
Discrimination Phase was a double-blind, single-dose, IV, 2 treatment, 2-period, 2-sequence, randomized
crossover design. Subjects received single IV doses of
the following treatments separated by a minimum 24-hour washout period:

44

Reference ID: 4739635

– Treatment X: d-MPH API 15 mg, IV.
– Treatment Y: Matching placebo, IV
HAP PD assessments were performed at different times after the administration of study drug. Safety
assessments were performed throughout and at the end of the Drug Discrimination Phase. A subject was
eligible for the Treatment Phase only if all eligibility criteria for Drug Discrimination based on the HAP
pharmacodynamic assessments were met.
Treatment Phase: After subjects passed and completed the Drug Discrimination Phase, they were eligible
to enter into the Treatment Phase. The Treatment Phase was a double-blind, single-dose, IV, 3-treatment,
3-period, 6-sequence, randomized crossover design, with the following treatments:
– Treatment A: KP415 API 30 mg, IV (molar equivalent for d-MPH to d-MPH API 15 mg dose;
KP415 API – Test Product)
– Treatment B: d-MPH API 15 mg, IV (Control or Reference Product)
– Treatment C: Matching placebo IV formulation.
HAP PD assessments and PK samples were collected at different times after the administration
of study drug. Safety assessments were performed throughout and at the end of the Treatment
Phase.
Follow-Up Visit: 7 ±2 days after administration of the last dose of study drug in the Treatment Phase,
subjects attended a Follow-Up Visit to have safety parameters evaluated.
4.1.3. Abuse potential measures and endpoints of interest
The PD measures of interest in the Treatment Phase were Drug Liking, Take Drug Again, Overall
Drug Liking, Feeling High, Good Effects, Bad Effects, Any Effects, and Alertness/Drowsiness
VAS scores, as well as ARCI-A and ARCI-BG scores.
The primary PD endpoint was Drug Liking VAS Emax.
The secondary endpoints in the Treatment Phase were as follows:
Drug Liking VAS (percent reduction in Drug Liking VAS Emax; time of occurrence of Emax [TEmax];
minimum effect [Emin]; time of occurrence of Emin [TEmin]; and area under the effect versus time curve
from time 0 to time t [AUE0-t], where t includes all times post-dosing at which effects were measured)
Global Effects: Take Drug Again VAS (Emax; key secondary endpoint) and Overall Drug Liking VAS
(Emax and Emin)
Positive effects: Feeling High VAS and Good Effects VAS (Emax, TEmax, AUEs)
Negative effect: Bad Effects VAS (Emax, TEmax, AUEs)
Other effects: Any Effects VAS (Emax, TEmax, AUEs)
Stimulant effects: Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, Addiction Research Center Inventory Amphetamine scale
(ARCI-A), and ARCI Benzedrine Group scale (ARCI-BG) (Emax, TEmax, Emin, TEmin, AUEs, as
applicable)

45

Reference ID: 4739635

4.1.4. Study subjects
A total of 199 subjects were screened for Part B, five of whom were originally screened for Part A,
but were reconsented for Part B. Of these, 69 eligible subjects were randomized to the Drug
Discrimination Phase. A total of 60 (87.0%) subjects completed the Drug Discrimination Phase, and 9
(13.0%) were discontinued. Five (7.2%) subjects were discontinued due to AEs, and 4 (5.8%) subjects
were discontinued for “other” reasons, including family emergency (1 subject), noncompliance (1
subject), subject request (1 subject), and investigator decision due to event of elevated blood pressure (1
subject). Of the 60 subjects who completed the Drug Discrimination Phase, 21 subjects did not meet
eligibility criteria to enter the Treatment Phase (Appendix 16.2.2.2.2 in the report), two subjects withdrew
consent, four subjects were discontinued early, and two subjects were discontinued as enrollment was
met. All subjects were included in the Qualification Safety Population.
A total of 31 subjects were randomized to the Treatment Phase. Thirty (96.8%) subjects completed the
study, and 1 (3.2%) subject was discontinued for “other” reasons (family emergency). All subjects were
included in the Safety Population and the 30 completers were included in the Completers Population and
PP Population.
4.1.5. Statistical methodologies used for the primary and key secondary endpoints
A mixed-effects analysis of covariance (ANCOVA) model for a crossover study was used to compare PD
endpoints (e.g., Emax, Emin, AUEs) between treatments. The model included treatment, period and
treatment sequence as fixed effects, and baseline (pre-dose) measurement as a covariate where applicable,
and subject nested within sequence as a random effect. A first-order direct carryover effect from the
treatment of previous period was included in the model if it was found to be significant at the 25% level.
For the first treatment period, KP415 API was used for all subjects and all PD endpoints. For endpoints
analyzed using the mixed effects model, least squares (LS) means, along with 95% confidence intervals
(CIs) (or 90% CIs, for the secondary endpoint comparisons of KP415 API 30 mg vs. placebo), were
provided for each treatment, and LS mean differences and associated CIs were provided for each pairwise
comparison.
For all endpoints, the assumption of normality that is required for the ANCOVA model was
examined using the Shapiro-Wilk Normality test on the residuals from the mixed-effect model.
For endpoints that did not meet the normal distribution assumption, the differences between
treatments were evaluated for symmetric distribution. In case it was found to be symmetric, a
paired t-test was performed. Otherwise, the Friedman’s method was used to test the overall
treatment effect, and the Sign test was used to compare the pairwise treatment differences. The
median differences and associated two-sided CI were estimated using the method of
Hodges-Lehman. The derived parameters TEmax and TEmin parameters were analyzed using the nonparametric method.
Primary and Key Secondary Endpoints
The primary analyses were based on testing the hypotheses below for Drug Liking VAS Emax, with
margins defined as follows: δ1 = 15 points, δ2 = 10 points, and δ3 = 11 points, and using one-sided 95%
CIs (Due to certain limitations of conducting one-sided analyses in SAS, a two sided test was performed

46

Reference ID: 4739635

to calculate 90% CIs if a primary analysis required a paired t-test or Sign test. The resulting lower limit
[superiority test] or upper limit [noninferiority test] of the
two-sided 90%CI was then compared with the respective margin and the p-value was divided by
2 to determine statistical significance relative to α=0.05 consistent with a one-sided test with a
one-sided 95% CI). The tests were conducted sequentially in the order listed below, and thus, no
multiplicity testing was needed. The key secondary endpoint, Take Drug Again VAS Emax was
analyzed in the same manner.
Secondary Endpoints
Secondary endpoint comparisons for d-MPH API versus placebo and d-MPH API versus
KP415 API were performed as two-sided hypothesis tests at a significance level of α=0.05 and
reported with 95% CI. Secondary endpoint comparisons for KP415 API vs placebo were
performed as two-sided hypothesis tests at a significance level of α=0.10 and reported with 90%
CI. The larger α in the latter comparison reduces the Type II error (decreasing the probability of
falsely assuming KP415 API and placebo have similar effects), and increases the power of this
test.
4.1.6.
•

•

•

Sponsor’s Pharmacodynamic Results
The mean difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax (primary endpoint) between d-MPH API 15 mg
and placebo was 30.5 and the lower bound of the 90% CI was greater than 15 (δ1; p<0.001);
therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected and the validity of the study was confirmed. The
median difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between d-MPH API 15 mg vs KP415 API 30 mg
was 29.0 and the lower bound of the 90% CI was greater than 10 (δ2; p=0.001); therefore, the
null hypothesis was rejected. These results demonstrate that compared to d-MPH API 15 mg,
KP415 API 30 mg (equimolar dose of d-MPH) was significantly less liked by recreational
stimulant users. The median difference in Drug Liking VAS Emax between KP415 API 30 mg vs
placebo was 0.5 and the upper limit of the 90% CI was less than 11 (δ3; p=0.001); therefore, the
null hypothesis was rejected. These results suggest that the subjective drug liking of KP415 API
30 mg was similar to that of placebo.
The mean difference in Take Drug Again VAS Emax (key secondary endpoint) between d-MPH
API 15 mg and placebo was 49.1 and the lower limit of the 95% CI was 35.2, which was greater
than 15 (δ1; p<0.001); therefore, the null hypothesis was rejected, indicating that subjects were
more willing to take d-MPH API 15 mg again compared with placebo. The mean difference
between d-MPH API 15 mg and KP415 API 30 mg was 43.1 and the lower limit of the 95% CI
was 29.2, which was greater than 10 (δ2; p<0.001); therefore, subjects were significantly less
willing to take KP415 API 30 mg compared with d-MPH API 15 mg. The mean difference
between KP415 API 30 mg and placebo was 6.0 and the upper limit of the 95% CI was 19.9,
which was greater than 11 (δ3; p=0.275). Since the CI contained 11 (δ3), the null hypothesis was
not rejected, indicating that subjects were more willing to take KP415 API 30 mg again compared
with placebo. However, a median difference of 6 points on a unipolar 100-point VAS is small and
may not be clinically meaningful.
For the majority of derived Emax, Emin, and AUEs for measures of global effects, positive
effects, and other effects (other secondary endpoints), d-MPH API 15 mg had statistically

47

Reference ID: 4739635

significantly higher values compared with both KP415 API 30 mg and placebo, and there was no
statistical difference between KP415 API 30 mg and placebo. Cumulative measures Drug Liking
VAS AUE0-7hr and AUE0-12hr were statistically higher for KP415 API 30 mg as compared
with placebo (p=0.099), but overall, KP415 API 30 mg had effects similar to placebo.

4.3.2.2. Statistical testing
For examining the abuse potential of 30 mg KP415 API through IV administration, the reviewer studied
the following comparisons:
1. 15 mg d-MPH API versus Placebo ( study validation)
2. 15 mg d-MPH API versus 30 mg KP415 API (relative abuse potential)
3. 30 mg KP415 API versus Placebo (absolute abuse potential)
Corresponding hypotheses for these comparisons on the primary endpoint, Drug Liking Emax, are listed
below:
1. 𝐻0 : 𝜇𝑑−𝑀𝑃𝐻 − 𝜇𝑃 ≤ 15 vs. 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝑑−𝑀𝑃𝐻 − 𝜇𝑃 > 15
2. 𝐻0 : 𝜇𝑑−𝑀𝑃𝐻 − 𝜇𝐾𝑃415 ≤ 10 vs. 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝑑−𝑀𝑃𝐻 − 𝜇𝐾𝑃415 > 10
3. 𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐾𝑃415 − 𝜇𝑃 ≥ 11 vs. 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐾𝑃415 − 𝜇𝑃 < 11
The significance level for each test is 5%.
The same statistical model and analysis method as Studies KP415-a01 and KP415-a02 were used in this
study.
The normality assumption of the model was not satisfied for Drug Liking Emax. The p-value of the Wtest on residuals was 0.0009. The normality of the paired differences was examined. Table 16 provides
the skewness and p-value of the W test for the paired differences for Drug Liking Emax.

4.3.3.2. Statistical testing
Based on the protocol prespecified analysis, the analysis for the key secondary endpoint, Take Drug
Again Emax, was planned the same as that for the primary endpoint, Drug Liking Emax. However, the
sponsor did not take unipolar visual analog scale of this measure into account. The prespecified
hypotheses were:
1. 𝐻0 : 𝜇𝑑−𝑀𝑃𝐻 − 𝜇𝑃 ≤ 15 vs. 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝑑−𝑀𝑃𝐻 − 𝜇𝑃 > 15
2. 𝐻0 : 𝜇𝑑−𝑀𝑃𝐻 − 𝜇𝐾𝑃415 ≤ 10 vs. 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝑑−𝑀𝑃𝐻 − 𝜇𝐾𝑃415 > 10
3. 𝐻0 : 𝜇𝐾𝑃415 − 𝜇𝑃 ≥ 11 vs. 𝐻𝑎 : 𝜇𝐾𝑃415 − 𝜇𝑃 < 11
The significance level for each test was 5%.
For the key secondary endpoint Take Drug Again Emax, Levene’s test for homogeneity of variance was
not significant (p= 0.8059), the first-order-carryover effect was not significant (p= 0.9096) at 𝛼 = 0.25,
and the Shapiro-Wilk W-test on the residuals was not significant for Drug Liking Emax (p=0.10721).
Therefore, the mixed effects model with period, sequence, and treatment as fixed effects and subject as a
random effect was used in the reviewer’s analysis.

4.4. Conclusion
The primary analysis demonstrated that the mean of Drug Liking Emax scores produced by 30 mg KP415
API was significantly smaller than that by 15 mg d-MPH API with a test margin of 10 points. (p<0.0001);
and the median difference in maximum liking between 30 mg KP415 API and placebo was significantly
smaller than 11 points (p=0.0040). The mean difference in maximum liking between 15 mg d-MPH API
and placebo was significantly greater than 15 points (p<0.0001). Thus, the study was validated.
The key secondary endpoint, Take Drug Again Emax, was on a unipolar visual analog scale. Unlike
studies KP415-a01 and KP415-a02, in which Take Drug Again VAS was on a bipolar visual analog scale
and the test margins for secondary endpoints were zero, the same test margins used for Drug Liking Emax
(bipolar) were used in the analysis on this key secondary endpoint in this study. In other words, the
prespecified test margins did not take unipolar scale of this measure into account. The secondary analysis
on the key secondary endpoint demonstrated significantly smaller mean produced by 30 mg KP415 API
than that by 15 mg d-MPH with a test margin of 10 points (p=0.0001) but failed to demonstrate no
significant mean difference between 30 mg KP415 API and placebo with a test margin of 11 points
(p=0.2750). However, descriptive statistics on secondary measures Any Effects VAS,
Alertness/Drowsiness VAS, Bed Effects VAS, Good Effects VAS, High VAS, and Overall Drug Liking
VAS and on their Emax scores all supported the findings from the primary analysis.
In conclusion, Study PK415-a03 demonstrated that with IV administration, 30 mg KP415 API has less
abuse potential than 15 mg d-MPH API and did not have abuse potential compared to placebo.

57

Reference ID: 4739635

5. Appendix
5.1. Oral Study
5.1.1. Statistical testing for High Emax
The model considered for High Emax was a mixed effects model with period, sequence, treatment, and
first-order-carryover as fixed effects and subject as a random effect, and predose response as a covariate.
However, the covariate was not significant. The reviewer examined data. Among 45 subjects, only two
subjects’ predose responses were 1 to one of five treatments, and all other predose responses were zero.
Therefore, the covariate was dropped from the model. The W-test for normality of residuals was not
significant (p= 0.7817). The Levene’s test for homogeneity of variances was also not significant
(p=0.1103). However, the first-order-carryover effect was highly significant (p=0.0006). The reviewer
examined heat map by sequence for High Emax.

(b) (6)

Figure 30: Heat map by sequence for High Emax
The heat map clearly shows that no first-order-carryover effect from any treatment to placebo except 120
mg KP415 prodrug (See blue rectangular). By examining the data, these subjects responded to 120 mg
KP415 prodrug with an Emax score of zero, and to the next treatment, placebo, with an Emax score
greater than or equal to 16. The situation is similar to what observed for Drug Liking Emax in this study.

5.2. Intranasal study
5.2.1. Statistical testing for High Emax
The model considered for High Emax was the mixed effects model with period, sequence, and treatment,
as fixed effects, predose response as a covariate, and subject as a random effect. However, the W-test for
normality on residuals was significant (p=0.0002). Thus, the normality of paired differences was further
examined for each comparison. The results from the W-test are listed in Table 26.

5.3. Intravenous study
5.3.1. Statistical testing for High Emax
The model considered for High Emax was the mixed effects model with period, sequence, and treatment,
and the first-order-carryover as fixed effects and subject as a random effect, because the first-ordercarryover effect was significant (p= 0.1408) at 0.25 significant level, and predose responses to High VAS
from all subjects were zero. However, the W-test for normality on residuals was significant (p<0.0001).
Thus, the normality of paired differences was further examined for each comparison. The results from the
W test are listed in Table 29.
